# Correction ledger — frequency-weighted reinforcement

Design per Kam (2026-08-03, voice): *"similar to when people learn, frequency is
used for reinforcement — if a mistake happens once, that could be isolated; if it
happens more than once, an increased weight is given."*

## The weight scale

Weight = number of occurrences. Each recurrence escalates the response:

| w | Meaning | Required response |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Isolated** — could be noise | Log it. Write a lesson file only if it would change future behaviour (importance filter). |
| 2 | **Reinforced** — a pattern, not noise | Lesson file mandatory. If one existed and didn't fire, diagnose *why* (wrong file? too abstract? bad retrieval handle?) and fix the lesson, not just the mistake. |
| ≥3 | **Regression** — the system is failing to learn | Treat like a failing test (no-skip rule). Automatic promotion candidate: move the rule into `identity/` or into *enforcement* (launcher check, hook, CLAUDE.md line). Raise it with Kam at the next briefing. |

Rules:
- Same *underlying* mistake in a new costume still increments the weight — match
  on root cause, not surface form.
- Weights never decay automatically. They are only retired at weekly
  consolidation, with the reasoning written in the audit note.
- Positive reinforcement counts too: when Kam explicitly praises a behaviour,
  log it as `praise` — repetition there tells us what to *keep* doing.

| 2026-09-08 | 🟠 **WEDNESDAY TOLD KAM A DRIVE SYNC WAS 'ADDITIVE, SO NOTHING IS AT RISK' — and 'additive' only rules out DELETIONS, not OVERWRITES.** Starting a DevMASTER→T9 rsync with no `--delete`, the sentence to Kam was *"it can add and update but cannot delete anything"*, framed as safety. **True and insufficient: `rsync -a` transfers whenever size or mtime differ and does NOT prefer the newer side, so a stale source overwrites a newer destination.** The live risk was concrete — `DevMASTER/TUESDAY` is a stale staging clone (HEAD `8105f925`) and `T9/TUESDAY` was the current one (`83eac5c9`, and it alone had `Launch_Tuesday.command`). **Self-caught ~10 minutes later while checking something else, and MEASURED rather than reasoned: `rsync -a -n -i` on that leg reported ZERO files would transfer**, so the shared files were identical and the hole was empty. **The error was never in the world; it was in the confidence.** Disclosed to Kam unprompted in the same message as the next instruction. | correction | +1 (representations family; the SAFETY-PROPERTY-STATED-WIDER-THAN-CHECKED costume) | [[2026-08-26_never-delete-cleanup-means-quarantine]] (deletion is the famous hazard, which is exactly why 'no deletions' reads as 'safe') · [[2026-08-05_verify-the-chain-not-the-legs]] (verify the artefact at the destination) · [[2026-09-04_decisions-held-narration-drifted]] (no characterisation without its measurement in the same breath — 'nothing is at risk' is a characterisation) · [[2026-08-16_classification-is-the-field-that-grants-authority]] (a safety word is a measurement) | **RULE: a safety claim names the property it actually checked. | 2026-09-08 | 🟠 **WEDNESDAY INVENTED A PLAUSIBLE ASYMMETRY AND HANDED IT TO AN AGENT AS A RULE INSIDE A BRIEF — the exact thing Wednesday forbids agents from doing to Wednesday.** The addendum told s150: *"a CLEAN mark on that surface is worthless, a DIRTY mark is assertive and probably real."* It sounds right — this morning's lesson was that GitHub renders an UNCOMPUTED `mergeable_state` as CLEAN, so CLEAN is the direction that lies. **From that true fact Wednesday derived a false one: that the OTHER direction must therefore be trustworthy.** s150 measured all nine PRs on Peter's list: **all three DIRTY marks were wrong** — two were clean and one was closed. **A stale snapshot is stale in every direction; staleness has no preferred sign.** The agent's replacement is better and is adopted verbatim: ***that surface is a snapshot; measure everything on it.*** **Zero cost — the agent measured all nine anyway, because the brief also told it to; had the brief carried only the heuristic it would have skipped the three CLEAN rows and missed that two were already CLOSED.** | correction | +1 (representations family; the DERIVED-ASYMMETRY costume — a true observation about one direction generalised into a licence for the other) | [[2026-08-14_i-read-representations-they-read-sources]] (rule 3: never state a mechanism not read — including a mechanism's failure MODE) · [[2026-09-08_a-false-absence-is-usually-my-own-instrument]] (the source lesson, over-generalised) · [[2026-09-07_a-census-complete-over-a-frame-that-is-not]] (the true fact was complete over CLEAN and silent about DIRTY) · [[2026-08-13_headline-must-match-the-operative-case]] | RETRACTED to the agent in the next mail, scoped to the heuristic only and not to the measurement instruction it rode with. **RULE: a claim about how an instrument FAILS is itself a measurement — one observed failure direction licenses nothing about the other.** |
| 2026-09-08 | 🟠 **WEDNESDAY RAN A WRITE-ONLY TOOL WITH `--help` AND POSTED THE WORD TO KAM'S READING SURFACE — he read it, thought Wednesday was in trouble, and asked what was wrong.** Probing `chat_reply.sh --help` for its usage, the script has NO usage guard: its only mode is to append its argument to the panel. So `--help` went up as a message from Wednesday, the panel's autoplay read it aloud, and Kam replied *"I got a message from you saying help. What do you need?"* and then *"I got a little bit worried. I thought you needed help."* **The root cause is a habit, not a typo: `--help` is treated as universally safe, and for a tool whose ONLY action is to write it is a live invocation.** **The attempted REPAIR was the more interesting half and it was refused, correctly.** Wednesday edited the entry's text in place to a correction note, keeping the original in a `superseded_text` field — a quarantine, not a delete. `chat_streams.py` then REFUSED to rebuild: its orphan detector keys on `(ts, text)`, so an entry whose text was CORRECTED in the stream reads as an entry the rebuild would DELETE. **The guard was right in spirit and its key is one field too strict.** Wednesday reverted the edit rather than reaching for `--harvest` (which would have re-filed `--help` as a fresh message) or weakening the guard — the correction went to Kam as a new message instead, which is what it should have been from the start: **do not rewrite history on the principal's surface; append.** | correction | 1 (NEW: a write-only tool probed with `--help`) · +1 (chat_streams orphan guard: a CORRECTED entry is indistinguishable from a LOST one) | [[2026-08-15_a-gui-open-is-a-write]] (an action classified as reading that writes — the same shape, pointed at a CLI flag) · [[2026-08-26_never-delete-cleanup-means-quarantine]] (the repair honoured it; the guard still refused) · [[2026-09-07_a-prior-ruling-gate-refusal-is-a-research-prompt]] (a refusal is a question — answered by reverting, not by overriding) · [[2026-08-20_dashboard-shows-only-live-work]] | **RULE: before passing any flag to a tool in `2_Project_Files/tools/`, read its arg parser — a tool with no usage guard has no safe probe.** Corrected to Kam in the same minutes, leading with it. Guard key `(ts,text)` recorded, NOT patched: narrowing it to `ts` would let a silent text-swap through, which is worse than a false refusal. |
| 2026-09-08 | 🟡 **A POINTER TAP FAILED SILENTLY BECAUSE WEDNESDAY PASSED A PANE ID WHERE THE TOOL WANTS A PANE NAME — and the first run's rc was never read, so it looked like nothing happened at all.** `cockpit.sh say %171 …` returned rc 1 with no visible output; the pane was unchanged and the agent kept waiting. **`say` resolves its inbox from `inbox_routing.conf`, which is keyed on the pane NAME (`Secuura/Blockchain`), so `grep -F '%171|'` matched nothing and `die` fired.** Diagnosed by re-running with the rc captured on its own line and then `bash -x` — not by re-reading the script, which is where the eye goes and where the defect was not. **This is the runtime-id lesson in a new costume: that file says a handover records a mechanism by its PATH, never by a pane id — and here Wednesday fed a pane id to the mechanism ITSELF.** A pane id is correct for `tmux capture-pane` and wrong for `cockpit.sh say`, which is exactly the kind of adjacency that makes it easy. **Zero cost — the mail was already verified at the destination, so nothing false was delivered anywhere; the agent simply waited ~4 minutes longer.** | correction | 1 (NEW: a runtime pane id passed to a NAME-keyed tool) · +1 (never-discard-stderr: the first invocation's rc was not captured) | [[2026-09-07_a-mechanism-is-recorded-by-its-path-not-its-runtime-id]] (the parent — recorded here as its INVOCATION half) · [[2026-08-06_never-discard-stderr]] (rc on its own line is what turned an invisible failure into a one-minute diagnosis) · [[2026-08-26_zsh-has-no-pipestatus]] (`| tail` also ate the `die` message) · [[2026-09-01_a-tap-is-a-pointer-not-a-message]] | FIXED: re-tapped with `"Secuura/Blockchain"`; the `--mail` freshness gate verified the mail at 04:41:56Z and reported `delivered (prompt clear)`. s150 consumed it and is amending #907. **Standing: read the rc of every tap, and never `tail` a command whose failure message is the thing you need.** |
| 2026-09-08 | 🔴 **WEDNESDAY SILENCED THE SCRIPT THAT THE BROWSER'S OWN VOICE RUNS THROUGH — and it would have looked correct right up until nothing ever spoke again.** Kam: *"Can you change it so that only the browser speaks?"* Wednesday made `speak.sh` silent by default. **But the panel's voice does not come from the panel:** the page calls `/api/speak`, and the server runs `speak.sh`. The change would have silenced BOTH paths while appearing to do exactly what was asked — the failure would have surfaced only as an absence, hours later, with no error anywhere. Caught before shipping by asking what else invokes the thing being disabled; `SPEAK_FROM_SERVER=1` exempts the server's own call and all three branches were exercised (terminal SILENT · server SPEAKS · explicit override SPEAKS). **The rule: before disabling a mechanism, enumerate its CALLERS — a shared implementation behind two user-visible features means turning one off turns both off.** | correction | 1 | [[2026-08-07_enumerate-every-surface-before-done]] · [[2026-09-08_the-check-ran-and-was-not-checking-the-thing]] (its sibling: a change that LOOKS like the feature working) | caught pre-ship; 3 branches proven |

Wednesday reported that back to him as the state. **GitHub computes `mergeable_state` ON DEMAND — a first read returns `unknown`, and the extranet was rendering unknown as clean.** s150 forced the computation and re-polled: **#895 and #896 are `dirty` — merge conflicts.** It also found **#900's base is #899's branch, not `develop`**, so merging it alone moves nothing on the trunk. **Wednesday's one correct instinct was refusing to recommend approval without the hold reason** — but it passed the word CLEAN through unexamined, and a rendered field from a third-party surface is a representation, not a measurement. | correction | 1 | [[2026-08-14_i-read-representations-they-read-sources]] · [[2026-09-08_a-false-absence-is-usually-my-own-instrument]] (an unknown rendered as a green) | corrected to Kam before he clicked anything |
| 2026-09-08 | 🟢 **THE HOLD REASON WAS RECORDED ALL ALONG — in the previous seat's HANDOVER and its history entry, verbatim — and Wednesday's inherited pickup did not carry it.** Three PRs were left unapproved because they are **18,609 added lines** (5,297 + 8,827 + 4,485 — s150 checked the arithmetic against the '~18,600' and it is exact), *"not reviewable to approval depth; carried to Kam as a shortfall rather than stamped."* Wednesday knew only THAT they were held, not WHY, and said so to Kam rather than guessing — which was right, but the answer was two files away. **The lesson is about WHICH ARTEFACT TO REACH FOR: a pickup is a summary and drops reasons; the handover and `history.md` keep them.** When a successor finds a hold with no reason, the reason is usually recorded — in the artefact the pickup was summarised FROM. | correction | 1 | [[2026-08-16_a-recorded-blocker-is-not-a-boundary]] (a recorded blocker is a claim — this is its mirror: an UNrecorded reason usually exists elsewhere) · [[2026-09-05_a-relayed-ruling-is-delivered-only-when-it-is-in-the-artefact]] | reason recovered, quoted to Kam, hold confirmed still standing |
| 2026-09-08 | 🔴 **SIXTH INSTANCE OF THE TAP-WITH-NO-MAIL FAMILY — AND THE FIRST WHERE THE ENFORCEMENT WENT GREEN WHILE THE RULE WAS BROKEN.** Wednesday tapped s150 with genuinely new CONTENT — a pane id (`%172`), a launch time, and a claim that a card had gone to Kam — and satisfied `cockpit.sh say --mail` by citing an ANSWER sent **33 minutes earlier** that contained none of it. **The w=5 enforcement verifies that a mail with that subject EXISTS, not that it CARRIES the tap's claims**, so it cannot fail for any tap citing any real prior mail — a check very close to one that cannot fail, built in direct response to this same family. **AGENT-CAUGHT, and its handling is the best of this class yet:** s150 acted on the WORK instruction (independently authorised by the cited ANSWER, DKIM-verified, which did set out the sequence) while explicitly refusing to treat the pane id, the time and the card as facts — *"a tap that cites a verifiable source can be acted on THROUGH the source, never on its own authority."* Adopted verbatim into the fleet method. **FIXED, not noted:** the gate now tests the property the rule was always about — AGE. A cited mail older than 15 minutes is REFUSED, because the rule is *a mail sent in the same action*; `SAY_ALLOW_OLD_MAIL=1` covers the genuine continuation and must be said aloud. | 2026-09-07 | **A COMMIT SUBJECT BEGINNING WITH `#` IS SILENTLY DISCARDED WHEN THE COMMIT IS COMPLETED BY `rebase --continue` — and the BODY's first line is promoted into its place, so the log looks fine.** Commit `335102d3`'s intended subject was *"#892 NO GO: the fix reproduced its own ticket's defect one level up"*. The log shows *"A green PROVISIONED writes a manifest…"* — the second paragraph. **Nothing errored and nothing warned.** **The precise mechanism, measured rather than assumed:** `git commit -F -` uses cleanup mode `whitespace`, which does NOT strip comment lines — which is why `#890 r2 GO-with-findings` (c1c04e37) and `#889 Finding 2 closed` (849b94c2) kept their subjects earlier the same day. **`rebase --continue` uses the EDITOR path, whose default cleanup DOES strip them.** Proven with a positive control: `git stripspace --strip-comments` on `#test subject\n\nbody line` returns `body line` alone. **So the same message survives one path and is mutilated by the other**, which is why it took a Secuura-style PR number to expose it. **How it was found is the other half:** Wednesday grepped the daily note for a phrase, got **0 hits**, and — instead of concluding the note was lost — **ran a control grep for a phrase it knew was there.** The control returned 1, so the instrument was fine and the search string was wrong. **That control is what turned a false alarm into a real finding**, and it is the same discipline that failed earlier today when `ls` and `head` were used to prove an absence. **The rule: never begin a commit subject with `#`** — put the PR number after a word (`PR #892 NO GO: …`) — **or pass `--cleanup=verbatim`.** **The general form, which is the part worth keeping: a message passed through a tool that INTERPRETS it can lose content with no error, and the loss is invisible because what remains still reads correctly.** Same family as the unquoted heredoc executing backticks and the `-q` flag suppressing the discriminating line. | correction | w+1 (prose destroyed in transit by a tool that interprets it) | [[2026-08-29_unquoted-heredoc-executes-backticks]] · [[2026-08-07_a-check-that-cannot-fail]] (the control that found it) | rule only; `335102d3`'s subject is not recoverable and history was NOT rewritten for a cosmetic loss |

| 2026-09-07 | **WEDNESDAY SPECIFIED A FIX MECHANISM WITHOUT MEASURING WHAT IT DID TO THE PROPERTY IN QUESTION — and the fix had the EXACT flaw Wednesday had just rejected the alternative for.** Ruling #890 round 2, Wednesday told the seat to replace `encodeURIComponent` with `Buffer.from(v,'utf8').toString('base64url')`, asserting it would remove the `URIError` **and** eliminate the `-` sentinel that made the scope join non-injective — and explicitly warned *"do not just add a try/catch: that fixes the 500 and leaves F3 standing."* **Three errors, all found by the seat, all measured:** (1) `base64url('-')` is the constant `LQ`, so the collision survives — **encoding a sentinel RENAMES it, it does not remove it**; (2) a total encoder ALONE trades the 500 for a **fresh collision class**, since every unpaired surrogate encodes to the same bytes (`b64('\ud800') == b64('\ud801') == b64('\ufffd') == "77-9"`) — **a new hole in the very property the PR exists to establish, which is Wednesday's own objection to the try/catch, applied to Wednesday's own fix**; (3) **the two instructions in that same mail contradicted each other** — the wire cell Wednesday specified (`tenantId:"\ud800"` → 400) **cannot pass** under Wednesday's own fix, which returns 200. **The self-check attestation on that mail PASSED and did not catch it, because the check compared ticket ids and numbers, not MECHANISM against SPECIFIED CELL.** Zero cost — the seat measured instead of complying, and the standing line *"if an instruction from Wednesday looks wrong, say so"* is what produced it. **The rule this earns: when a brief names a specific fix MECHANISM (not just a requirement), the mechanism is a claim about the product and needs the same provenance as a count — either measure what it does to the property, or say "a candidate shape, unmeasured — verify before adopting." the fix shape is a per-episode fire-once (reset `fcnt` on fire, or a `fired_$key` marker cleared when the hash changes). **🔴 MECHANISM RETRACTED AND CORRECTED 2026-09-07 20:4x (same day, by the seat that wrote the first half of it), on the FOURTH re-fire:** the account above was composed from the leg's own lines WITHOUT READING LINE 30, which they depend on — **`STATE_DIR="$(mktemp -d ...)"` is a PER-INVOCATION temp dir**, so `cnt`, `fcnt` and the hash do NOT persist across invocations. **What actually happens: the script loops internally (`sleep $INTERVAL` at `:217`) and EVERY fire path is `exit 0` — which kills the invocation and DESTROYS that state.** The runner restarts it, the counters reset to zero, and `STABLE_N` samples later the same condition is re-derived from scratch. **So there IS a reset-after-fire — it is the process exit — and the true defect is sharper than 'no fire-once': THE MECHANISM THAT RESETS THE COUNTER IS THE SAME MECHANISM THAT FIRES THE ALERT, so a fire can never suppress its own repeat.** **This VOIDS the fix shape both rows recorded:** a `fired_$key` marker in `$STATE_DIR` would be wiped by the very exit that wrote it. **The correct shape is the pattern already working in the same file** — a PERSISTENT marker under `$CTX_STATE` (`cockpit/state/`) keyed by pane AND capture hash, cleared when the hash changes, exactly as the ctx legs use `ctx_fired_${gen}_NN`. **Retraction SCOPE, stated so it takes nothing true with it:** both rows' operational conclusions STAND — the alert is true about the render and false about the situation; leg (a) runs first so a repeat cannot mask a real wake; and not editing this file while something depends on it is still right, now better founded because the fix is not the one-liner either row called it. **Handled instead by making the triage CHEAP** — the prescribed order collapsed into one combined command (inbound-only inbox + detector + shell count), rather than three calls per alert. **Wednesday is at 72% and a 75-minute alert storm at 1-2% per triage is a real path into the rotation band on noise alone**, which is the honest reason this is recorded rather than shrugged at.
